Yes. **We can send to Slack without holding a Slack API key** by using an incoming webhook authorized for a channel in the user’s workspace. ## Assistant

_Thinking:_



For a small in-house system, I recommend **one always-on application on one host**, rather than a set of cloud services.

The same application would serve the alert-setup and admin pages, periodically collect events, match them against saved alerts, and send notifications. It would keep alert rules, source progress, and pending deliveries in a **persistently stored SQLite database**. Email would use an authenticated SMTP sender; Slack would use authorized incoming webhooks. Keeping delivery methods behind separate adapters would let us add channels later without changing event matching.

My preferred form is a **single container on an existing always-on machine**, if you have one, or a small VPS if you do not. It would need persistent storage, backups, protected credentials, and authenticated access to the web pages. The application should record deliveries so a restart or retry does not casually send the same alert twice. We do **not** need Postgres, Redis, Kubernetes, or a separate frontend to begin with.

| Where it runs | Main tradeoff |
| --- | --- |
| Existing home/office server | Least new infrastructure, but alerts depend on its power and internet connection. |
| Small VPS | More dependable access, but has a recurring cost and needs basic maintenance. |
| Scheduled serverless functions | No always-on machine, but adds storage, scheduling, and delivery-service complexity; not my first choice here. |

This is a **recommendation for review**, not a selected stack or a frozen plan. What machine or hosting environment, if any, could you keep running continuously for this?

## Assistant

GitHub is a good place for the **code**, but [GitHub Pages hosts static sites](https://docs.github.com/en/pages/getting-started-with-github-pages/what-is-github-pages). It cannot keep a C# server running, poll feeds, update SQLite, or send alerts. GitHub Actions can run scheduled jobs, but it is not a persistent host for an interactive alert service.

**My recommendation:** keep the project on GitHub and deploy one small **ASP.NET Core application** from it to an always-on host. That application can serve a simple frontend for alert setup **and the admin view**, run collection and matching, and store state in the SQLite database you approved. Visitors would open a URL—no local build or hosting required. A small VPS is one possible host; we have not chosen one yet.

Your C# proposal fits this well. I would start with server-rendered pages rather than a separate frontend application. SMTP and Slack incoming webhooks also fit, with one important distinction: **no event-source API key is required**, but SMTP credentials and Slack webhook URLs are still secrets that must be configured and protected.

If you mean “try it out” without letting visitors create real alerts or send messages, GitHub Pages could instead host a **static demo**. That is cheaper to expose, but it would not demonstrate the working service.

When someone opens the GitHub link, do you want them to **create a real alert and receive a notification**, or explore a demo without live delivery?

## Assistant

No—your proposal is **not far-fetched**. It is a sensible lightweight direction for an in-house application. The key distinction is that the script makes the app easy to **start locally**; the computer running it must remain on for alerts to keep arriving.

| Requirement | Lightweight proposed form |
| --- | --- |
| Where it runs | On the user’s Windows or Linux machine, inside Docker. |
| Build → test → run | Root `run.sh` and `run.ps1` invoke the same container configuration. Tests run before startup; a failure stops the launch. GitHub Actions is not needed. |
| Alert setup and admin view | One C# ASP.NET Core application serves both sets of pages—no separate frontend service. |
| Collection and matching | A background process in that same application polls feeds and evaluates saved alerts. |
| Persistent data | Your approved SQLite choice, stored in a Docker volume so restarting or rebuilding does not erase alerts. |
| Email, Slack, later channels | SMTP and Slack webhook senders behind a common delivery boundary. Credentials and webhook URLs still need private configuration. |

That is **one application container, not a cluster**. Re-running the script should reuse the existing data, avoid launching a second poller, and not resend previously handled events merely because it restarted. Running tests on *every* launch is feasible as you requested, though it makes startup slower; build caching can help.

The unavoidable setup is Docker and the sender credentials. Here is the **full original-requirement coverage matrix**, including delivery and admin—not just event sources. “Agreed” means we have a planning direction, **not a working application**.

| Original requirement | Where we stand | Details still to plan or validate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Users set up alerts** | A local frontend is agreed; SQLite will persist each person’s alerts. No alert form or rule engine exists. | What users can select for each event type; editing, pausing, and deleting rules; concrete examples of when each rule matches. |
| **Breaking news worldwide** | The approved coverage matrix includes war, politics, diplomacy, health, AI/security, science and other consequential topics. BBC, NYT, Al Jazeera and Guardian feeds returned sample stories; Ars Technica and Nature returned relevant samples. | Define “breaking” versus analysis or repeat coverage; test geographic/language gaps, story grouping, freshness, and source-use conditions. |
| **Market movements** | Keyless samples returned crypto prices, daily FX, and one index. | Decide which instruments and moves users can alert on; find dependable, documented coverage beyond those samples; set freshness expectations. |
| **Natural disasters worldwide** | GDACS, NASA EONET, WHO and IFRC returned events across several types. Worldwide coverage across major types and regions remains the goal. | Check the type-by-region gaps, source timeliness, event updates, and what counts as a major event. |
| **“Something important” and other event kinds** | The category/source matrix is approved as a direction. Its 0–1 scores are illustrative estimates for representative major events, **not per-story scores**. | Define checkable matching criteria without assuming one universal importance score; decide how another event category joins the system. |
| **Users get notified** | Local polling, matching, and persisted delivery state fit the agreed architecture; none is implemented. | Decide notification timing, duplicate/update handling, retries, and what happens while a person’s computer is off. |
| **Email and Slack** | SMTP and Slack incoming webhooks are agreed. Event-source API keys are not required; delivery credentials and webhook URLs still are. | Decide how a local user configures and tests destinations, and how failures and secrets are handled. |
| **More channels later** | Extensibility is required; separate delivery adapters are a proposed way to achieve it. | Agree on the boundary and a concrete acceptance check—for example, adding a channel without changing event matching. |
| **Admin view** | A frontend containing an admin view is agreed. Its contents and access rules are not. | Decide what it shows and permits: source health, delivery failures, retries, configuration, and protection of admin functions. |
| **Where and how it runs** | Agreed: each person runs a local, containerized C# copy with SQLite. Scripts **in the repository’s top-level directory** will build → test → run on Windows and Linux; GitHub Actions is off. No elevated script privileges are implied. | Specify the container/startup layout, Docker prerequisites, persistent-volume backup, private configuration, and checks for idempotent restarts. No scripts exist yet. |

**Bottom line:** the runtime direction and event taxonomy are substantially settled. The largest remaining *product-planning* gap is the journey from **creating a rule → deciding an event matches → sending one useful notification**. Separately, sampled feeds still need operational coverage validation; approval of the matrix did not prove worldwide completeness. The detailed plan remains unfrozen.

Would you like to define that alert-creation and matching journey next?

Implementation:

```csharp
using Microsoft.Data.Sqlite;

var builder = WebApplication.CreateBuilder(args);

var databasePath = builder.Configuration["WorldAlerts:DatabasePath"];
if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(databasePath))
{
    databasePath = Path.Combine(builder.Environment.ContentRootPath, "data", "worldalerts.sqlite");
}

var database = new SqliteDatabase(Path.GetFullPath(databasePath));
database.TryInitialize();

builder.Services.AddSingleton(database);

var app = builder.Build();

app.MapGet("/api/health", (SqliteDatabase db) =>
    db.IsAvailable
        ? Results.Ok(new HealthResponse("ok"))
        : Results.Json(new HealthResponse("unavailable"), statusCode: StatusCodes.Status503ServiceUnavailable));

app.Run();

internal sealed record HealthResponse(string Status);

internal sealed class SqliteDatabase
{
    private readonly string _path;
    public SqliteDatabase(string path) => _path = path;
    public bool IsAvailable { get; private set; }

    public void TryInitialize()
    {
        try
        {
            var directory = Path.GetDirectoryName(_path);
            if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(directory)) Directory.CreateDirectory(directory);
            using var connection = new SqliteConnection(new SqliteConnectionStringBuilder { DataSource = _path, Mode = SqliteOpenMode.ReadWriteCreate }.ToString());
            connection.Open();
            using var command = connection.CreateCommand();
            command.CommandText = "CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS SchemaVersions (Version INTEGER NOT NULL); SELECT 1;";
            command.ExecuteNonQuery();
            IsAvailable = true;
        }
        catch (Exception)
        {
            IsAvailable = false;
        }
    }
}

public partial class Program { }
```

```python
import http.server, time, struct, zlib
class H(http.server.BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
    def do_GET(self):
        if self.path.startswith('/slow'):
            time.sleep(3)
            body = b'\x89PNG...'
            ...
        else:
            html = b'''<html><body style="margin:0"><iframe src="http://127.0.0.1:4200/" style="border:0;width:1280px;height:860px"></iframe><img src="/slow" style="position:fixed;left:-10px;top:-10px;width:1px;height:1px"></body></html>'''
```

```csharp
using Microsoft.Data.Sqlite;

var builder = WebApplication.CreateBuilder(args);

var configuredPath = builder.Configuration["WorldAlerts:DatabasePath"];
var databasePath = string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(configuredPath)
    ? Path.Combine(builder.Environment.ContentRootPath, "data", "worldalerts.sqlite")
    : configuredPath;

var database = new SqliteDatabase(Path.GetFullPath(databasePath));
database.TryInitialize();
builder.Services.AddSingleton(database);

var app = builder.Build();

app.MapGet("/", () => "World Alerts API");
app.MapGet("/api/health", async (SqliteDatabase db, CancellationToken cancellationToken) =>
    await db.CanConnectAsync(cancellationToken)
        ? Results.Ok(new HealthResponse("ok"))
        : Results.Json(new HealthResponse("unavailable"), statusCode: StatusCodes.Status503ServiceUnavailable));

app.Run();

internal sealed record HealthResponse(string Status);

/// <summary>
/// Owns the local SQLite file. Initialization failure is recorded, not thrown, so the API still starts
/// and reports itself unhealthy without disclosing the configured path.
/// </summary>
internal sealed class SqliteDatabase
{
    private readonly string _path;
    private readonly string _connectionString;

    public SqliteDatabase(string path)
    {
        _path = path;
        _connectionString = new SqliteConnectionStringBuilder
        {
            DataSource = path,
            Mode = SqliteOpenMode.ReadWriteCreate,
            DefaultTimeout = 5,
        }.ToString();
    }

    public void TryInitialize()
    {
        try
        {
            EnsureDirectory();
            using var connection = new SqliteConnection(_connectionString);
            connection.Open();
            using var command = connection.CreateCommand();
            command.CommandText =
                """
                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS SchemaVersions (
                    Version INTEGER NOT NULL,
                    AppliedUtc TEXT NOT NULL
                );
                """;
            command.ExecuteNonQuery();
        }
        catch (Exception exception)
        {
            // Unusable database: health checks report unavailability instead of failing startup.
            Console.Error.WriteLine($"SQLite initialization failed: {exception.GetType().Name}");
        }
    }

    private void EnsureDirectory()
    {
        var directory = Path.GetDirectoryName(_path);
        if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(directory))
        {
            Directory.CreateDirectory(directory);
        }
    }

    public async Task<bool> CanConnectAsync(CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        try
        {
            await using var connection = new SqliteConnection(_connectionString);
            await connection.OpenAsync(cancellationToken);
            await using var command = connection.CreateCommand();
            command.CommandText = "SELECT 1";
            return await command.ExecuteScalarAsync(cancellationToken) is not null;
        }
        catch (Exception)
        {
            return false;
        }
    }
}

public partial class Program;
```

Fastest path is the API only (no frontend needed yet). From `/prod/sonrisa_homework`:

## 1. Fresh ingest

```bash
rm -rf src/api/data                       # start from an empty database
dotnet run --project src/api --urls http://127.0.0.1:5000
```

Wait ~10–20 s for the startup poll (the log prints `News feed <id> polled: parsed N, inserted N, duplicates N, skipped N` for all six feeds), then in a second terminal:

```bash
curl -s http://127.0.0.1:5000/api/health
curl -s 'http://127.0.0.1:5000/api/news?limit=2'
```

Expect `{"status":"ok"}` and a JSON array of stories with `sourceId`, `title`, `link`, `publishedUtc`, `firstSeenUtc`, `family`, `categories`, `provenance`.

## 2. Confirm all six sources landed

```bash
curl -s 'http://127.0.0.1:5000/api/news?limit=200' | python -c "
import json,sys,collections
d=json.load(sys.stdin)
print(len(d),'items'); print(collections.Counter(i['sourceId'] for i in d))
print('missing title/link:', sum(1 for i in d if not i['title'] or not i['link']))
"
```

You should see `bbc-world`, `nyt-world`, `aljazeera-all`, `guardian-world`, `ars-technica`, `nature` — Nature is the interesting one (RSS 1.0, day-precision dates).

## 3. Idempotency across restart

Stop with `Ctrl+C`, then run the same start command again. Rows must not double, and every feed should log `inserted 0, duplicates N`. Optionally:

```bash
python -c "
import sqlite3; c=sqlite3.connect('src/api/data/worldalerts.sqlite')
print('rows', c.execute('select count(*) from NewsItems').fetchone()[0])
print('dupes', c.execute('select count(*) from (select SourceId,NativeId,count(*) n from NewsItems group by 1,2 having n>1)').fetchone()[0])
"
```

Expect `dupes 0`.

## 4. Two quick negative checks

Broken database → unhealthy, no path leak:

```bash
rm -rf src/api/data   # optional
WorldAlerts__DatabasePath=/proc/1/blocked/events.sqlite dotnet run --project src/api --urls http://127.0.0.1:5001
curl -s -i http://127.0.0.1:5001/api/health   # 503 {"status":"unavailable"}, path absent
```

Polling off → zero outbound fetches:

```bash
WorldAlerts__News__PollingEnabled=false dotnet run --project src/api --urls http://127.0.0.1:5002
```

The log should contain no `https://feeds...` requests, and `/api/news` serves whatever was already stored.

## 5. Optional frontend check

```bash
npm --prefix src/web start
```

Open `http://127.0.0.1:4200` — the shell should show `Backend: Available` (it only checks health; the news list has no UI until task 23).

Two things that are *not* bugs: older items appear (BBC carried stories back to 18 Oct — 24-hour filtering is task 18), and the same story from two outlets appears twice (cross-source clustering is task 13). If anything differs from the above, tell me the command and output and I'll have DeepSeek fix it.

## Live request samples

**World news**
```bash
curl -s "http://127.0.0.1:5000/api/news?limit=1"
```
```json
{
 "sourceId": "guardian-world",
 "title": "Helen Haines live updates: police charge accused Brendan Nugent with additional offences...",
 "publishedUtc": "2026-10-21T02:08:23+00:00",
 "family": "world-news",
 "provenance": "https://www.theguardian.com/world/rss"
}
```

**Diplomacy**
```bash
curl -s "http://127.0.0.1:5000/api/news?limit=200"   # filter family == diplomacy
```
```json
{
 "sourceId": "un-press",
 "title": "Tensions ‘Eroding Trust’ in Kosovo, Mission Head Warns Security Council...",
 "publishedUtc": "2026-10-20T23:55:15+00:00",
 "family": "diplomacy"
}
```

## Manual checks

### Readable news from the last 24 hours

Paste this into your terminal. It filters using actual timestamps rather than assuming every returned item is recent:

```bash
curl -fsS 'http://127.0.0.1:5000/api/news?limit=200' |
python3 -c '
import sys,json
from datetime import datetime,timezone,timedelta
now=datetime.now(timezone.utc)
for x in json.load(sys.stdin):
    p=x.get("publishedUtc")
    if not p: continue
    t=datetime.fromisoformat(p.replace("Z","+00:00"))
    if now-timedelta(hours=24) <= t <= now:
        print(f"{p} | {x[\"sourceName\"]}")
        print(x["title"])
        if x.get("summary"): print(x["summary"])
        print(x["link"], "\n")
'
```

**Important:** this shows recent stories, not confirmed important stories. The current running process does **not** expose the new summary field yet; those changes await testing and restart.

### Inspect market and disaster metadata

```bash
curl -fsS 'http://127.0.0.1:5000/api/markets?source=binance-btcusdt-ticker&limit=2' |
python3 -m json.tool

curl -fsS 'http://127.0.0.1:5000/api/disasters?source=gdacs&limit=2' |
python3 -m json.tool

curl -fsS 'http://127.0.0.1:5000/api/advisories?limit=2' |
python3 -m json.tool
```

Check provider timestamps, source attribution, magnitude/alert level, and delayed-market labels. CISA entries are **advisories**, not proof of current incidents.

### Save and retrieve a subscription

This writes a disabled test subscription; it sends nothing:

```bash
curl -fsS -X POST 'http://127.0.0.1:5000/api/subscriptions' \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"eventType":"world-news","channel":"email","destination":"manual-test@example.invalid","enabled":false}'
```

Then retrieve it:

```bash
curl -fsS 'http://127.0.0.1:5000/api/subscriptions' |
python3 -m json.tool
```

Repeat the POST: it should retain the same subscription ID rather than create another row. Remove it afterward using its returned ID:

```bash
curl -i -X DELETE 'http://127.0.0.1:5000/api/subscriptions/ID'
```

### Test subscription matching

```bash
curl -fsS -X POST http://127.0.0.1:5001/api/subscriptions \
```

```bash
curl -fsS http://127.0.0.1:5001/api/alerts/selection |

```

This **does not send email or queue notifications**.

## User

what is the importance score baseline? 

---

## Assistant

There are **two different baselines**:

- **Market-price baseline:** the earlier price used to calculate a move—currently an exact 24-hour comparison. Missing history prevents scoring that move.
- **Importance score:** a deterministic **0.0–1.0 heuristic** based on metadata strength. It is **not a probability**, and there is currently **no universal score cutoff** for alerts.

Alert eligibility still uses explicit rules:
- Crypto: **≥5% over 24h**
- FX: **≥1% daily**
- Indices: **≥2% daily**
- Disasters: approved severity/magnitude/tsunami rules
- News: source, consequential-topic triggers or independent corroboration

The displayed score explains/ranks rule strength; **the score alone does not decide whether an alert is sent**. That distinction should be made clearer in the interface.

---

## User

values closer to 1 should have high importance. meaning > 0.7 is important event and anything below is ignored (no notification or feed publication). is this a bad direction? my idea

---

## Assistant

**That’s a reasonable direction**—one score threshold shared by the feed and notifications is clearer than today’s separate score and importance flag.

I’d implement it as:

- **Score > 0.70:** important; appears in the feed and can trigger subscribed notifications.
- **Score ≤ 0.70:** excluded from both.
- Freshness, valid provider timestamps, subscription matching and duplicate suppression remain separate gates.
- The debug view explains the score’s contributing factors.

The main risk is **calibration**: `0.80` must mean comparable significance across news, disasters and markets. Simply thresholding today’s heuristic scores could hide genuinely important events. We should recalibrate them against concrete positive/negative examples, retaining the approved market and hazard boundaries as anchors.

The score would express **estimated importance—not certainty that the report is true**.
